BÖLMƏ 1: TİBB VƏ SAĞLAMLIĞ
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1.1 HUMAN ANATOMY — İNSAN ANATOMİYASI
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Ürək-damar sistemi:
Ürək dörd kameralı bir orqandır: sağ və sol qulaqcıq (atrium), sağ və sol mədəcik (ventrikul). Sağ tərəf ağciyər dövriyyəsinə, sol tərəf böyük dövriyyəyə xidmət edir. Ürək dəqiqədə orta hesabla 60–100 dəfə vurur. Sinüs düyünü (SA node) elektrik impulsu yaradaraq ürəyin ritmini təyin edir. AV düyünü bu impulsu mədəciklərə ötürür. Koronar arterlər ürək əzələsini qan ilə təmin edir: LAD (sol ön enən), LCX (sol sirkumfleks), RCA (sağ koronar). Ürək çatışmazlığı sol və ya sağ ürəyin pompalama funksiyasının zəifləməsidir.

[RU] Дыхательная система:
Лёгкие состоят из правого (три доли) и левого (две доли) лёгкого. Альвеолы — функциональные единицы газообмена. Диафрагма — главная дыхательная мышца. Жизненная ёмкость лёгких у взрослого — около 4,8 л. Сурфактант снижает поверхностное натяжение альвеол, препятствуя их спаданию. Бронхиальное дерево делится: трахея → главные бронхи → долевые → сегментарные → терминальные бронхиолы → альвеолы. Контроль дыхания осуществляется продолговатым мозгом через хеморецепторы (CO₂, O₂, pH).

[EN] Nervous System Overview:
The central nervous system (CNS) comprises the brain and spinal cord. The peripheral nervous system (PNS) includes somatic and autonomic divisions. The brain is divided into: cerebrum (cognition, motor, sensory), cerebellum (coordination, balance), brainstem (vital functions: breathing, heartbeat). Neurons communicate via action potentials and synaptic transmission. Neurotransmitters: dopamine (reward, movement), serotonin (mood), acetylcholine (muscle contraction, memory), norepinephrine (fight-or-flight), GABA (inhibition), glutamate (excitation). The blood-brain barrier (BBB) selectively regulates molecular exchange between bloodstream and CNS.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1.2 XƏSTƏLIKLƏR VƏ DİAQNOSTİKA
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Diabetes Mellitus (Şəkər xəstəliyi):
Tip 1 diabet: Pankreas beta hüceyrələrinin autoimmun destruksiyası nəticəsində insulin istehsalı tamamilə dayanır. Əsasən uşaqlarda və gənclərdə baş verir. Müalicə: insulin terapiyası (bazal + bolus). Tip 2 diabet: İnsulin rezistansı ilə başlayır, zamanla insulin sekresiyası azalır. Risk faktorları: piylənmə, fiziki hərəkətsizlik, irsi meyllilik, yaş (>45). Müalicə: metformin (birinci xətt), SGLT-2 inhibitorları, GLP-1 agonistləri, sulfonilurea, insulin. HbA1c hədəfi: <7% (ümumi populyasiya üçün). Kəskin ağırlaşmalar: hipoglikemiya, diabetik ketoasidoz (DKA), hipersmolar hiperqlikemik vəziyyət (HHS). Xronik ağırlaşmalar: nefropatiya, retinopatiya, neyropatiya, makrovaskulyar xəstəlik.

[RU] Гипертоническая болезнь:
Артериальная гипертензия — систолическое АД ≥ 140 мм рт. ст. и/или диастолическое ≥ 90 мм рт. ст. Классификация: нормальное (<120/80), повышенное (120–129/<80), 1 степень (130–139/80–89), 2 степень (≥140/≥90). Первичная (эссенциальная) — 90–95% случаев, причина неизвестна. Вторичная — реноваскулярная, эндокринная (феохромоцитома, гиперальдостеронизм), медикаментозная. Лечение: ИАПФ (эналаприл, лизиноприл), БРА (лозартан, валсартан), бета-блокаторы, тиазидные диуретики, блокаторы кальциевых каналов (амлодипин). Цель: <130/80 мм рт. ст. при высоком риске. Осложнения: инфаркт миокарда, инсульт, сердечная недостаточность, хроническая болезнь почек.

[EN] Infectious Diseases:
Bacterial vs. Viral: Bacteria are prokaryotes with cell walls; treated with antibiotics. Viruses are non-living particles requiring host cell machinery; treated with antivirals or vaccines. Key bacterial pathogens: Staphylococcus aureus (MRSA: methicillin-resistant), Streptococcus pneumoniae (pneumonia, meningitis), Mycobacterium tuberculosis (TB: first-line RIPE = rifampin, isoniazid, pyrazinamide, ethambutol), Escherichia coli (UTI, sepsis). Key viral pathogens: HIV (targets CD4+ T-cells, treated with ART: NRTI + NNRTI + PI), Hepatitis B (HBsAg, anti-HBs, HBeAg markers), Hepatitis C (DAAs achieve >95% cure), SARS-CoV-2 (ACE2 receptor entry, mRNA vaccines). Sepsis criteria (Sepsis-3): organ dysfunction + suspected infection; treat with early antibiotics, IV fluids, vasopressors (norepinephrine first-line).

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1.3 DƏRMANLAR VƏ FARMAKOLOGİYA
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Antibiotiklər:
Beta-laktamlar (penisilinlər, sefalosporinlər, karbapenemler): hüceyrə divarının sintezini bloklayır. Aminoglikozidlər (gentamisin, amikacin): ribosomun 30S alt vahidini bloklayır, gram-mənfi bakteriyalara qarşı effektiv, nefrotoksik. Floroquinolonlar (siprofloksasin, levofloksasin): DNA gyrase inhibitoru, geniş spektrli. Makrolidlər (azitomisin, klaritomisin): 50S ribosomun inhibitoru, atipik mikroorqanizmlərə qarşı effektiv. Vankomisinin: MRSA müalicəsinin əsasını təşkil edir, hüceyrə divarı sintezini inhibə edir. Antibiotik rezistansı mexanizmləri: beta-laktamaza istehsalı, efluks pompası, hədəf modifikasiyası, membran keçiriciliyinin azalması.

[RU] Анальгетики и НПВП:
Парацетамол (ацетаминофен): ингибирует ЦОГ в ЦНС, жаропонижающий и анальгетик, гепатотоксичен при передозировке (антидот: N-ацетилцистеин). НПВП (ибупрофен, диклофенак, напроксен): неселективные ингибиторы ЦОГ-1 и ЦОГ-2, противовоспалительные, анальгетики, жаропонижающие. Побочные эффекты: ЖКТ-кровотечение, почечная недостаточность, сердечно-сосудистые риски. Опиоиды (морфин, кодеин, оксикодон, фентанил): агонисты µ-опиоидных рецепторов, сильные анальгетики. Риски: угнетение дыхания, зависимость. Антагонист: налоксон (налоксон при передозировке). ВАШ (визуально-аналоговая шкала) 0–10 для оценки боли.

[EN] Cardiovascular Pharmacology:
Statins (atorvastatin, rosuvastatin): HMG-CoA reductase inhibitors, reduce LDL cholesterol, first-line for cardiovascular prevention. Side effects: myopathy, rhabdomyolysis (rare), elevated liver enzymes. ACE inhibitors (lisinopril, ramipril): block conversion of angiotensin I → II, reduce preload and afterload, first-line for HFrEF and diabetic nephropathy. Contraindicated in pregnancy. ARBs (losartan, valsartan): block AT1 receptor, similar to ACEi but less cough. Beta-blockers (metoprolol, carvedilol, bisoprolol): reduce HR, BP, and myocardial oxygen demand; first-line post-MI and HFrEF. Anticoagulants: warfarin (VKA, INR monitoring), DOACs (rivaroxaban, apixaban, dabigatran — no routine monitoring needed), heparin (UFH: APTT monitoring; LMWH: anti-Xa). Antiplatelet: aspirin (irreversible COX-1 inhibitor), clopidogrel (P2Y12 inhibitor), ticagrelor.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
1.4 CƏRRAHİYYƏ VƏ PROSEDURLar
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Ümumi cərrahiyyə prinsipləri:
Sterillik: cərrahi alətlər 134°C-də avtoklav ilə sterilizasiya edilir. Sezmək (insizyon): kəsik toxumanı minimal travma ilə açmalıdır. Hemostatika: elektrokoaqulyasiya, ligatüralar, hemostatik dərmanlar (traneksamik turşusu). Laparoskopik cərrahiyyə: minimal invaziv, CO₂ ilə pnevmoperitoneum yaradılır, trokar və kamera vasitəsilə icra edilir. Üstünlükləri: az ağrı, tez sağalma, kosmetik effekt. Anesteziya növləri: ümumi (entubsiya + inhalasion anesteziya), regional (spinal/epidural), yerli. Postoperativ ağırlaşmalar: infeksiya, tromboemboliya (DVT/PE profilaksiyası: LMWH, kompressiya corabları), anastomoz sızması, atelektaz.

[RU] Нейрохирургия:
Краниотомия — хирургический доступ к головному мозгу путём временного удаления части черепа. Показания: опухоли мозга, субдуральные и эпидуральные гематомы, аневризмы, АВМ. Глиобластома (GBM, WHO Grade IV): самая агрессивная первичная опухоль мозга. Лечение: резекция + темозоломид + лучевая терапия. Медиана выживаемости: ~15 месяцев. Гидроцефалия: накопление ЦСЖ, лечится вентрикулоперитонеальным шунтированием. Спинальная хирургия: дискэктомия (удаление грыжи диска), спондилодез (стабилизация позвоночника), ламинэктомия (декомпрессия спинного мозга).

[EN] Emergency Medicine:
ATLS (Advanced Trauma Life Support) Primary Survey — ABCDE: Airway (maintain with c-spine protection), Breathing (assess bilateral chest expansion, O₂ sat), Circulation (hemorrhage control, IV access, fluid resuscitation), Disability (GCS, pupils, focal neuro deficit), Exposure (undress + environmental control). Tension pneumothorax: deviated trachea, absent breath sounds, hypotension — needle decompression 2nd ICS MCL, then chest tube. Massive hemorrhage: permissive hypotension (MAP ~50 mmHg), 1:1:1 ratio RBC:FFP:platelets, tranexamic acid within 3 hours. Burns: Rule of Nines (head 9%, each arm 9%, each leg 18%, trunk 36%, perineum 1%). Fluid resuscitation: Parkland formula = 4 mL × weight(kg) × %TBSA burned (half in first 8h, half in next 16h).

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
BÖLMƏ 2: HÜQUQ
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2.1 CİVİL HÜQUQ (MÜLKİ HÜQUQ)
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Müqavilə hüququ əsasları:
Müqavilənin yaranması üçün 4 əsas element tələb olunur: 1) Teklif (offer) — bir tərəfin müəyyən şərtlər əsasında müqavilə bağlamaq istəyini ifadə etməsi; 2) Qəbul (acceptance) — digər tərəfin bu təklifi şərtsiz qəbul etməsi; 3) Əvəzlik (consideration) — hər iki tərəfin bir şey verməsi/almağı vəd etməsi; 4) Hüquq qabiliyyəti (capacity) — tərəflərın 18 yaşından yuxarı olması, ağıl sağlamlığı. Müqavilənin pozulması (breach): tam pozulma, qismən pozulma, əvvəlcədən imtina (anticipatory breach). Hüquq müdafiəsi vasitələri: zərərin ödənilməsi (kompensasiya zərərlər, itirilmiş mənfəət), xüsusi icra (specific performance), müqavilənin ləğvi (rescission). Azərbaycan Mülki Məcəlləsi müqavilələri tənzimləyir: hədiyyə, alqı-satqı, icarə, podrat, borc, kredit müqavilələri.

[RU] Гражданская ответственность (деликтное право):
Деликт — гражданское правонарушение, влекущее ответственность. Элементы деликта: 1) Обязанность (duty of care); 2) Нарушение обязанности (breach); 3) Причинно-следственная связь (causation): фактическая (but-for test) и юридическая (proximate cause); 4) Ущерб (damages). Виды ущерба: компенсаторные (реальный ущерб, упущенная выгода, моральный вред), штрафные (punitive damages — при умышленном или грубо небрежном поведении). Виновность: умысел (intent), халатность (negligence), строгая ответственность (strict liability — без вины). Срок исковой давности: в РФ общий — 3 года. Исковая давность прерывается признанием долга.

[EN] Contract Law — Common Law vs. Civil Law:
Common Law (UK, US, Australia): Contracts require offer, acceptance, consideration, and intention to create legal relations. Consideration must be present (past consideration is insufficient). Doctrines: promissory estoppel (reliance without consideration), frustration (supervening impossibility), misrepresentation (false statement inducing contract). Damages: expectation (put in position as if contract performed), reliance (reimburse costs), restitution (prevent unjust enrichment). Civil Law (France, Germany, Azerbaijan, most of Europe and Latin America): Based on codified statutes (civil codes). No consideration requirement — agreement + cause (causa). Contracts may be voided for: error (mistake), dol (fraud), violence (duress), lésion (gross inequality). Force majeure clauses are more broadly recognised. CISG (UN Convention on International Sale of Goods): applies to cross-border commercial sales between 95+ contracting states.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2.2 CİNAYƏT HÜQUQU
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Cinayətin elementləri:
Cinayətin 4 elementi: 1) Hüquqa zidd əməl (actus reus) — qanunla qadağan edilmiş fəaliyyət və ya hərəkətsizlik; 2) Cinayət niyyəti (mens rea) — qəsdən hərəkət etmək (dolus directus), ehtiyatsızlıq (culpa), həlakətvarlıq; 3) Səbəbiyyət əlaqəsi — əməl ilə nəticə arasında birbaşa bağlılıq; 4) Zərər — şəxsə, əmlaka və ya cəmiyyətə zərər. Cinayətin növləri (Azərbaycan CM): Böyük ictimai təhlükə törədən (ağır — 5-10 il; xüsusilə ağır — 10+ il), az ağır, qeyri-ağır. Cəza növləri: cərimə, ictimai iş, azadlıqdan məhrumetmə, ömürlük azadlıqdan məhrumetmə (Azərbaycanda ölüm cəzası 1998-cı ildən ləğv edilib). Cinayəti istisna edən hallar: zəruri müdafiə, zərurət vəziyyəti, əqli qüsur.

[RU] Уголовный процесс в РФ:
Стадии уголовного процесса: 1) Возбуждение уголовного дела; 2) Предварительное расследование (следствие или дознание); 3) Подготовка к судебному разбирательству; 4) Судебное разбирательство; 5) Постановление приговора; 6) Апелляция и кассация. Принципы: презумпция невиновности, право на защитника, недопустимость доказательств, полученных с нарушением закона. Меры пресечения: подписка о невыезде, залог, домашний арест, заключение под стражу. Виды наказаний по УК РФ: штраф, обязательные/исправительные работы, ограничение свободы, принудительные работы, лишение свободы, пожизненное лишение свободы. Смертная казнь в России — мораторий с 1996 г.

[EN] Criminal Law — Anglo-American System:
Homicide categories: Murder (1st degree: premeditated, deliberate; 2nd degree: intentional, not premeditated), Manslaughter (voluntary: heat of passion; involuntary: criminal negligence). Felonies vs. misdemeanors: felonies (>1 year imprisonment), misdemeanors (<1 year). Beyond reasonable doubt: prosecution's burden (>90% certainty threshold informally). Defenses: insanity (M'Naghten: didn't know nature/wrongfulness of act), self-defense (reasonable, proportionate, necessary), duress, entrapment, alibi. Fourth Amendment (US): protects against unreasonable searches/seizures; exclusionary rule suppresses illegally obtained evidence. Miranda rights: right to remain silent, right to attorney, warnings required before custodial interrogation. Plea bargaining: ~97% of US federal convictions via guilty pleas.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2.3 TİCARƏT HÜQUQU VƏ KORPORATIV HÜQUQ
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Şirkət növləri:
Azərbaycanda əsas şirkət strukturları: Məhdud məsuliyyətli cəmiyyət (MMC) — ən geniş yayılmış forma, nizamnamə kapitalı pay şəklindədir, məsuliyyət nizamnamə kapitalı ilə məhdudlaşır. Açıq Səhmdar Cəmiyyət (ASC) — səhmlər birjada dövriyyədədir, daha ciddi açıqlıq tələbləri var. Qapalı Səhmdar Cəmiyyət (QSC) — səhmdar sayı məhduddur. Fərdi sahibkarlıq — sadə qeydiyyat, tam şəxsi məsuliyyət. Şirkətin idarəedilməsi (corporate governance): İdarə heyəti (board of directors), Müşahidə şurası, Audit komitəsi. Divident — mənfəətin paylaşdırılması. M&A (birləşmə və alınma): birləşmə (merger), alınma (acquisition), due diligence (hüquqi və maliyyə yoxlaması).

[RU] Корпоративное право РФ:
ООО (Общество с ограниченной ответственностью): участники несут риск убытков лишь в размере своих долей. Уставный капитал — минимум 10 000 руб. АО (Акционерное общество): ПАО (публичное) — акции обращаются на бирже, НАО (непубличное) — ограниченный круг акционеров. Органы управления: общее собрание акционеров (высший орган), совет директоров (наблюдательный), единоличный исполнительный орган (генеральный директор). Корпоративные договоры: акционерные соглашения — регулируют права голосования и порядок отчуждения акций. Банкротство: несостоятельность — неспособность исполнить денежные обязательства > 3 месяцев, сумма долга > 300 000 руб. (юр. лицо).

[EN] International Trade Law:
WTO (World Trade Organization): governs multilateral trade through agreements — GATT (goods), GATS (services), TRIPS (intellectual property). Most-favoured-nation (MFN) principle: treat all WTO members equally. National treatment: foreign goods same as domestic once imported. Dispute settlement: panels → Appellate Body → implementation/retaliation. INCOTERMS 2020: trade terms defining risk/cost transfer — EXW (Ex Works, buyer bears all costs), FOB (Free On Board, seller delivers to port), CIF (Cost Insurance Freight), DDP (Delivered Duty Paid, seller bears everything). Letters of Credit (LC): documentary LC under UCP 600 — bank guarantees payment upon compliant documents. Arbitration: ICC, LCIA, AAA, UNCITRAL Rules. New York Convention (1958): enforcement of foreign arbitral awards in 170+ countries.

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
2.4 ƏMƏK VƏ AİLƏ HÜQUQU
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Əmək hüququ:
Azərbaycan Əmək Məcəlləsi əsas müddəalar: Əmək müqaviləsi yazılı formada bağlanır, ən azı 2 nüsxədə. Sınaq müddəti 3 ayadək (mütəxəssislər üçün 6 ay). Əmək vaxtı: həftəlik 40 saatdan artıq olmamalıdır. Əlavə iş (iş saatından artıq) razılaşma ilə, iki qatdan az olmayan əmək haqqı ilə ödənilir. Məzuniyyət: ən azı 21 təqvim günü. Hamiləlik məzuniyyəti: 126 gün (70 doğuşaqədər + 56 sonra, çoxlu doğuşda 70+70). Əmək müqaviləsinin ləğvi: işçinin razılığı ilə, ixtisar, uyğunsuzluq, intizam pozuntusu. İşçilərin hüquqları: ədalətli ödəniş, təhlükəsiz iş şəraiti, həmkarlar ittifaqına üzvlük.

[RU] Семейное право РФ:
Заключение брака: взаимное согласие, достижение брачного возраста (18 лет, в исключительных случаях — 16). Брачный договор: может регулировать имущественные отношения. Общая совместная собственность супругов: всё, нажитое в браке (кроме дарения и наследства). Развод: в органах ЗАГС (при отсутствии детей и споров) или через суд. Раздел имущества: по ½ каждому (если нет брачного договора). Алименты: 1 ребёнок — 1/4 дохода, 2 ребёнка — 1/3, 3+ — 1/2. Лишение родительских прав: уклонение от обязанностей, жестокое обращение, алкоголизм/наркомания, хроническое злоупотребление правами.

[EN] Employment Law (US/EU):
US Federal Laws: Title VII of the Civil Rights Act (1964) — prohibits discrimination based on race, color, religion, sex, national origin. ADEA — age discrimination (40+). ADA — disability discrimination, requires reasonable accommodation. FMLA — 12 weeks unpaid leave for serious health conditions or family reasons (50+ employee threshold). FLSA — minimum wage ($7.25 federal, states may set higher), overtime >40h/week at 1.5x rate. EU Employment Law: Directive 2019/1152 — transparent working conditions. EU Working Time Directive: 48h max weekly average, 11h daily rest, 4 weeks annual leave. GDPR impacts HR: lawful processing of employee data, data minimisation, right to erasure limitations for employment records. At-will employment (US only): employer or employee can terminate for any/no reason except illegal discrimination.

BÖLMƏ 3: TEXNOLOGİYA VƏ PROQRAMLAŞDIRMA
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
3.1 PROQRAMLAŞDIRMA DİLLƏRİ
──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

[AZ] Python:
Python dinamik tipli, yüksək səviyyəli, interpretasiya olunan proqramlaşdırma dilidir. Guido van Rossum tərəfindən 1991-ci ildə yaradılmışdır. [EN] Systems Programming — C and Rust:
C Language: low-level, compiled, manual memory management. Pointers: int *ptr = &x — ptr holds address of x, *ptr dereferences. Stack vs heap: local variables on stack (auto-freed), malloc/calloc/realloc/free for heap. Buffer overflow: writing beyond allocated memory — undefined behavior, common security vulnerability. Undefined behavior (UB): dereferencing null/dangling pointer, integer overflow (signed), reading uninitialized memory. C standard libraries: <stdio.h>, <stdlib.h>, <string.h>, <math.h>. Rust: memory safety without GC via ownership system. Rules: each value has one owner, ownership transfers (move semantics), borrowing (&T immutable, &mut T mutable — only one mutable borrow at a time), lifetimes. No null: use Option<T> (Some(T) or None). No exceptions: use Result<T, E> (Ok(T) or Err(E)). Fearless concurrency: Send/Sync traits, Arc<Mutex<T>> for shared state.

Question: A triangle has sides of length 5, 12, and 13. Is it a right triangle? What is its area?

For the right triangle check, the Pythagorean theorem is the definitive test: if a² + b² = c² where c is the longest side, it's a right triangle. Here, 5² + 12² = 25 + 144 = 169 = 13². So yes, it's a right triangle. This is actually one of the well-known Pythagorean triples (5, 12, 13).

For the area, since it's a right triangle, the two legs serve as base and height, making the calculation straightforward: (1/2)(5)(12) = 30. I should also verify with Heron's formula to reinforce the concept and show the student a cross-check technique — that's good pedagogical practice for a tutoring context. With s = 15, Heron's formula gives sqrt(15 × 10 × 3 × 2) = sqrt(900) = 30. Confirmed.

Step 1: Check if it's a right triangle using the Pythagorean theorem.
For a right triangle with hypotenuse c: a^2 + b^2 = c^2.
The longest side is 13, so check: 5^2 + 12^2 = 25 + 144 = 169 = 13^2.
Yes, it satisfies the Pythagorean theorem.

Step 2: Confirm the triangle inequality (to ensure it's a valid triangle).
5 + 12 = 17 > 13. 5 + 13 = 18 > 12. 12 + 13 = 25 > 5. All satisfied.

Step 3: Calculate the area.
For a right triangle, the two legs are the base and height.
Area = (1/2) * base * height = (1/2) * 5 * 12 = 30.

Verification: Using Heron's formula: s = (5+12+13)/2 = 15.
Area = sqrt(15 * 10 * 3 * 2) = sqrt(900) = 30. Confirmed.

Therefore, the triangle is a right triangle with an area of 30 square units.

P(t) = P₀ × (1 + r)^t, where P₀ = 10,000, r = 0.05, t = 10.

P(10) = 10,000 × (1.05)^10.

[EN] Category: CODING
Question: What are the most important Linux signals and how do they work?
Response: <think>
The user is asking about Linux signals — this is a foundational systems programming topic that any DevOps engineer needs to understand deeply. I should cover the most commonly encountered signals, their default behaviors, and practical usage patterns.

I need to think about what matters most in a DevOps context. The critical signals are SIGTERM vs SIGKILL (graceful vs forced shutdown), SIGINT (Ctrl+C), SIGHUP (reload config convention), and SIGCHLD (child process management). I should include the signal numbers since people often use `kill -9` without knowing what signal 9 actually is.

For practical demonstrations, I should show both command-line usage with `kill` and programmatic signal handling in Python, since that's common in DevOps tooling. The container shutdown sequence is especially important — many production issues stem from applications not handling SIGTERM properly, leading to hard kills after the grace period. I should also mention the exec form vs shell form Docker CMD distinction, because that's a very common gotcha where signals get swallowed by the shell process instead of reaching the application. A table format will help organize the signals clearly, followed by practical code examples and the container lifecycle explanation.
</think>

Signals are asynchronous notifications sent to processes. They're how the OS and other processes communicate events like "please terminate" or "your child process finished."

**The signals you need to know:**

| Signal | Number | Default action | Meaning |
|--------|--------|----------------|----------|
| SIGHUP | 1 | Terminate | Terminal closed (also: reload config by convention) |
| SIGINT | 2 | Terminate | Ctrl+C pressed |
| SIGQUIT | 3 | Core dump | Ctrl+\ pressed |
| SIGKILL | 9 | Terminate | Force kill (CANNOT be caught or ignored) |
| SIGSEGV | 11 | Core dump | Segmentation fault (invalid memory access) |
| SIGTERM | 15 | Terminate | Polite termination request |
| SIGSTOP | 19 | Stop | Pause process (CANNOT be caught) |
| SIGCONT | 18 | Continue | Resume paused process |
| SIGCHLD | 17 | Ignore | Child process terminated |
| SIGUSR1/2 | 10/12 | Terminate | User-defined (apps choose meaning) |

**How they work in practice:**

```bash
kill -TERM 1234    # Send SIGTERM to PID 1234 (polite: "please shut down")
kill -9 1234       # Send SIGKILL (force: "die now, no cleanup")
kill -HUP 1234     # Send SIGHUP (by convention: "reload config")
```

**In Python:**
```python
import signal, sys

def graceful_shutdown(signum, frame):
    print(f"Received signal {signum}, shutting down gracefully...")
    # Close database connections, flush buffers, etc.
    sys.exit(0)

signal.signal(signal.SIGTERM, graceful_shutdown)
signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, graceful_shutdown)  # Ctrl+C
```

**The shutdown sequence that matters for containers:**

1. Docker sends SIGTERM to PID 1 in the container
2. Your app should catch SIGTERM, stop accepting new requests, finish in-flight requests, close connections, and exit cleanly
3. Docker waits (default 10 seconds) for the process to exit
4. If still running after the grace period, Docker sends SIGKILL (ungraceful, no cleanup)

If your Dockerfile uses `CMD ["python", "app.py"]` (exec form), Python is PID 1 and receives signals directly. If you use `CMD python app.py` (shell form), `/bin/sh` is PID 1 and swallows signals — your app never sees SIGTERM and gets SIGKILL'd after the timeout.

[EN] Category: CODING
Question: My `useEffect` is firing twice in dev and I'm losing my mind. I have:

```tsx
useEffect(() => {
  console.log('subscribing');
  const sub = client.subscribe(channel, onMessage);
  return () => sub.unsubscribe();
}, [channel]);
```

In dev I see 'subscribing' twice on mount. Production it's once. Is this a bug in React 18?
Response: <think>
This is the classic StrictMode double-invoke effect, and it's not a bug — it's intentional. The user's reaction ("is this a bug?") tells me they don't yet understand *why* React added this. The two-line fix would be "remove StrictMode" but that's the wrong advice; StrictMode catches real bugs in development. The right answer explains the *purpose* of the double-invocation: it surfaces effects that aren't idempotent / don't clean up properly, which become real bugs in future React features (transitions, offscreen rendering, etc.) where effects can be unmounted and remounted.

Now the user's specific code: `client.subscribe(channel, onMessage)` returns a subscription, the cleanup unsubscribes. On the surface this looks correct — and in fact, in production it IS correct because it only runs once. But in StrictMode dev, the sequence is:

1. subscribe (sub A)
2. unsubscribe (sub A)  -- via cleanup
3. subscribe (sub B)

If `client.subscribe` is genuinely idempotent and `unsubscribe` works, there's no bug — just two log lines. But if the subscription has side effects (e.g., increments a server-side ref count, or triggers a join message), then the user might be seeing real duplicates downstream.

The answer: this is StrictMode being helpful. If their `subscribe` is properly paired with `unsubscribe`, the double log is harmless and they should ignore it. If they're seeing actual duplicate behavior, the subscription system has a bug they need to fix. I should also mention the common related issue: `onMessage` likely changes identity each render, which would cause re-subscriptions on every render — they should check that. And `channel` being an object vs primitive matters for the dep array.

Finally, I should prepare for the inevitable follow-up: "how do I make my subscribe NOT do this?" — the answer is usually `useEffectEvent` (still experimental) or stable callbacks via `useRef`.
</think>

Not a bug — it's React 18 StrictMode intentionally double-invoking effects in development. The sequence is:

1. mount → effect runs (subscribe)
2. immediate fake unmount → cleanup runs (unsubscribe)
3. immediate remount → effect runs again (subscribe)

The purpose: surface effects that aren't properly cleaned up. React is preparing the ecosystem for features (Offscreen, transitions) where effects can legitimately be torn down and re-run while the component stays mounted. If your effect can't survive that round-trip without leaving garbage, you have a latent bug.

For your code specifically — if `client.subscribe` and `sub.unsubscribe()` are properly paired (calling unsubscribe really does cancel everything subscribe started), the double log in dev is harmless. Production won't double-fire. **Ignore it.**

If you're seeing actual duplicate *behavior* (two messages delivered for every event, server thinks you joined a room twice, etc.), that's your subscription system being non-idempotent, and StrictMode just exposed it. Fix the subscription system, not the effect.

One real bug worth checking in your snippet: dependencies.

```tsx
useEffect(() => {
  const sub = client.subscribe(channel, onMessage);
  return () => sub.unsubscribe();
}, [channel]);  // <-- onMessage missing
```

If `onMessage` is defined inline in the parent, it changes identity every render. ESLint's `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` would flag this. You probably want `onMessage` stable (defined outside, or wrapped in `useCallback`), or use a ref pattern so the effect doesn't re-subscribe whenever the callback changes.

[EN] Category: CODING
Question: Wait, ref pattern? I always thought refs in effects were a code smell. Show me what you mean.
Response: <think>
The user has internalized "refs in effects = bad" which is half right. The actual rule is: don't use refs to *escape* React's data flow (e.g., reading derived state from a ref instead of letting React re-render). But there's a legitimate, well-known pattern for handling "latest callback" in long-lived subscriptions, and it's specifically endorsed by Dan Abramov's writing on this. The React team has been working on `useEffectEvent` (formerly `useEvent`) precisely to formalize this pattern, but it's still experimental.

The pattern: store the latest callback in a ref, update the ref on every render, and have the effect read `ref.current` when it needs to invoke the callback. This decouples the subscription lifetime from the callback identity.

I should show the pattern with concrete code, explain when to use it (long-lived subscriptions where you want "the latest version of the callback" but don't want to tear down the subscription), and mention `useEffectEvent` as the future direction. I should also be honest about when NOT to use it: for short-lived effects where the cost of teardown is trivial, just include the callback in deps and let it re-subscribe.

Keep code focused, two examples — naive vs ref pattern — and a short discussion of `useEffectEvent`.
</think>

The "refs in effects are a smell" rule applies when you're using refs to dodge React's data flow (reading state via a ref to avoid re-rendering, etc.). The latest-callback pattern is different — it's specifically endorsed by the React team for the case you have: a long-lived subscription whose handler logic should always reflect the latest props/state, without tearing down and rebuilding the subscription on every render.

The naive version that re-subscribes constantly:

```tsx
function ChatRoom({ channel, onMessage }: Props) {
  useEffect(() => {
    const sub = client.subscribe(channel, onMessage);
    return () => sub.unsubscribe();
  }, [channel, onMessage]);  // re-subscribes on every render if onMessage isn't stable
}
```

The ref pattern:

```tsx
import { useEffect, useRef } from 'react';

function ChatRoom({ channel, onMessage }: { channel: string; onMessage: (m: Message) => void }) {
  const onMessageRef = useRef(onMessage);

  // Keep the ref in sync with the latest prop, every render
  useEffect(() => {
    onMessageRef.current = onMessage;
  });

  // Subscribe once per channel; the handler reads the latest callback at call time
  useEffect(() => {
    const sub = client.subscribe(channel, (msg) => {
      onMessageRef.current(msg);
    });
    return () => sub.unsubscribe();
  }, [channel]);

  return null;
}
```

Now the subscription only rebuilds when `channel` changes. The handler always calls the freshest `onMessage` because it dereferences the ref at message time, not at subscribe time.

When *not* to do this:
- Short-lived effects where re-subscribing is cheap. Just put the callback in deps.
- When the callback's identity is already stable (defined outside the component, memoized via `useCallback` with stable deps).

**Future direction:** `useEffectEvent` (still experimental as of React 19) is exactly this pattern, blessed and built-in:

```tsx
import { experimental_useEffectEvent as useEffectEvent } from 'react';

function ChatRoom({ channel, onMessage }: Props) {
  const handleMessage = useEffectEvent((msg: Message) => onMessage(msg));

  useEffect(() => {
    const sub = client.subscribe(channel, handleMessage);
    return () => sub.unsubscribe();
  }, [channel]);  // handleMessage is intentionally NOT in deps
}
```

When this stabilizes, prefer it. Until then, the ref pattern is the idiomatic workaround and won't get you yelled at in code review.

[EN] Category: CODING
Question: We had an incident last week where a single malformed JSON message in a Kafka topic caused our consumer to crash, restart, re-consume the same message, crash again - infinite loop for hours until someone noticed. Our 'DLQ' was a topic we wrote to manually but obviously the consumer never got that far. I want a real fix. Walk me through what poison-pill handling should actually look like.
Response: <think>
This is a really common operational issue and the user is right to want a systemic fix, not a one-off patch. Let me think about what actual poison-pill handling looks like.

The core failure pattern: a message arrives that the consumer cannot process (deserialization error, schema mismatch, business logic exception that's deterministic). The consumer throws, the message is not committed, the consumer is restarted (by k8s, supervisor, or just the next poll loop), it pulls the same message, throws again. Infinite loop. Offset doesn't advance, partition is blocked, lag grows.

The right architecture has several layers:

1. **Distinguish transient from permanent errors.** A network blip retrying is fine. A JSON parse error retrying forever is not. The application has to classify exceptions:
   - Transient: timeout, connection refused, 5xx from downstream → retry with backoff
   - Permanent: deserialization error, schema violation, business invariant violation → DLQ, advance offset

2. **Bounded retries with backoff for transient errors.** After N retries, treat as permanent and DLQ.

3. **DLQ that is unconditionally writable.** The DLQ producer must have its own simpler serializer (raw bytes preferred), its own producer instance, and must NOT depend on the same dependencies that might be failing for the main path. If your main consumer can't write to the DLQ because your shared Kafka producer is broken, you're back to the infinite loop.

4. **Pre-deserializer error handling.** Most poison-pill scenarios are deserialization errors. Kafka Streams has a built-in `DeserializationExceptionHandler` (LogAndContinueExceptionHandler / LogAndFailExceptionHandler / custom). For raw consumers, you have to handle this yourself. The trick: if your value deserializer throws, you don't even have a typed message yet, so your DLQ producer needs to handle raw bytes.

5. **Manual offset management.** You can't just rely on auto-commit because auto-commit doesn't know whether your processing succeeded. Use `enable.auto.commit=false` and commit explicitly after either successful processing or successful DLQ publication.

6. **Observability.** Every DLQ publish must emit a metric and a log line. Without this you discover poison pills via 'why is our backlog growing?' instead of an alert.

7. **DLQ schema.** The DLQ message should include: the original message bytes (so you can replay), the topic/partition/offset (so you can find it again), the exception class and message, the consumer instance ID, the timestamp.

8. **Replay tooling.** A poison-pill in the DLQ today might be a fixable bug tomorrow. You need a way to replay DLQ messages back to the main topic after a fix.

Let me give a concrete code example using the Java Kafka client (or Python). I'll use Python since it's more readable.

Also worth mentioning: parking the partition. If you can't write to the DLQ for some reason, the right behavior is NOT to advance the offset (data loss) but also NOT to retry forever (head-of-line blocking). One option is 'park the partition': stop consuming from this partition, alert, but keep consuming from other partitions. Implementing this requires manual partition assignment, not the consumer group protocol.

Spring Kafka has built-in support for this: `DeadLetterPublishingRecoverer` + `DefaultErrorHandler` with retry config. Worth mentioning but not the only approach.
</think>

The fix is structural - poison-pill handling is a property of your consumer architecture, not something you add at the edge. Here's the production-grade pattern.

**The four layers**

Every robust event consumer has these:

1. **Error classification** - is this transient or permanent?
2. **Bounded retries with exponential backoff** for transient errors
3. **Independent DLQ producer** that doesn't share fate with the main path
4. **Manual offset commits** - never advance until processing or DLQ-publishing succeeds

Skip any of these and you'll loop forever, lose data, or block partitions.

**The Python skeleton**

```python
import json
import logging
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Optional

from confluent_kafka import Consumer, Producer, KafkaError, Message

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class PermanentError(Exception):
    """Errors we should NOT retry: parse errors, schema violations, etc."""

class TransientError(Exception):
    """Errors we SHOULD retry: timeouts, 5xx, connection refused."""

@dataclass
class DLQRecord:
    original_topic: str
    original_partition: int
    original_offset: int
    original_value: bytes  # raw bytes - never failed to deserialize
    error_class: str
    error_message: str
    consumer_instance: str
    timestamp_ms: int

def make_consumer() -> Consumer:
    return Consumer({
        "bootstrap.servers": BROKERS,
        "group.id": "order-processor",
        "enable.auto.commit": False,            # we commit manually
        "auto.offset.reset": "earliest",
        "isolation.level": "read_committed",
    })

def make_dlq_producer() -> Producer:
    # Separate producer: minimal config, raw bytes, never shares fate.
    return Producer({
        "bootstrap.servers": BROKERS,
        "acks": "all",
        "enable.idempotence": True,
        "compression.type": "lz4",
        "linger.ms": 0,                         # ship immediately
    })

def process(msg: Message) -> None:
    """Business logic. Raises TransientError or PermanentError on failure."""
    try:
        payload = json.loads(msg.value())
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        raise PermanentError(f"malformed JSON: {e}") from e
    # ... validate schema, apply business rules, write to DB ...

def publish_to_dlq(producer: Producer, msg: Message, err: Exception, instance_id: str) -> None:
    record = DLQRecord(
        original_topic=msg.topic(),
        original_partition=msg.partition(),
        original_offset=msg.offset(),
        original_value=msg.value() or b"",
        error_class=type(err).__name__,
        error_message=str(err)[:1000],
        consumer_instance=instance_id,
        timestamp_ms=int(time.time() * 1000),
    )
    # Headers carry metadata; body is the original raw bytes for easy replay.
    headers = [
        ("original_topic", record.original_topic.encode()),
        ("original_partition", str(record.original_partition).encode()),
        ("original_offset", str(record.original_offset).encode()),
        ("error_class", record.error_class.encode()),
        ("error_message", record.error_message.encode()),
        ("consumer_instance", record.consumer_instance.encode()),
    ]
    producer.produce(
        topic=f"{record.original_topic}.dlq",
        value=record.original_value,
        headers=headers,
        key=msg.key(),
    )
    producer.flush(timeout=10.0)  # synchronous - don't advance offset until DLQ is durable

def run() -> None:
    consumer = make_consumer()
    dlq_producer = make_dlq_producer()
    consumer.subscribe(["orders"])
    instance_id = os.environ.get("HOSTNAME", "unknown")

    try:
        while True:
            msg = consumer.poll(timeout=1.0)
            if msg is None:
                continue
            if msg.error():
                if msg.error().code() == KafkaError._PARTITION_EOF:
                    continue
                log.error("consumer error: %s", msg.error())
                continue

            attempt = 0
            max_attempts = 5
            while True:
                try:
                    process(msg)
                    break  # success
                except TransientError as e:
                    attempt += 1
                    if attempt >= max_attempts:
                        log.warning("transient errors exhausted; treating as permanent: %s", e)
                        publish_to_dlq(dlq_producer, msg, e, instance_id)
                        metrics.dlq_published.labels(reason="transient_exhausted").inc()
                        break
                    backoff = min(2 ** attempt, 30)
                    log.info("transient error, retry %d/%d in %ds: %s", attempt, max_attempts, backoff, e)
                    time.sleep(backoff)
                except PermanentError as e:
                    publish_to_dlq(dlq_producer, msg, e, instance_id)
                    metrics.dlq_published.labels(reason="permanent").inc()
                    break
                except Exception as e:
                    # Unknown exception - treat as permanent to avoid infinite loop
                    log.exception("unexpected error; treating as permanent")
                    publish_to_dlq(dlq_producer, msg, e, instance_id)
                    metrics.dlq_published.labels(reason="unknown").inc()
                    break

            # Commit only after success OR successful DLQ publish.
            consumer.commit(message=msg, asynchronous=False)
    finally:
        consumer.close()
        dlq_producer.flush(30.0)
```

**Why each piece matters**

- **`enable.auto.commit=false`**: auto-commit can advance offsets while your code is still processing, causing data loss on crash. Manual commit after success is the only safe pattern.
- **Separate DLQ producer**: if you share a producer with your main pipeline, a producer-side failure (e.g., schema registry down, broker connection issue) breaks both paths simultaneously and you're back to the infinite loop.
- **`producer.flush()` before commit**: the DLQ publish must be durable *before* you advance the offset. Otherwise: DLQ publish queued, offset committed, pod crashes, message lost.
- **Catch-all `except Exception`**: deliberate. Unknown exceptions become permanent failures rather than infinite-loop hazards. You'd rather DLQ a few false positives than re-loop on a code bug.
- **Exception classification**: this is where teams cheap out and pay later. Be explicit about which exceptions are which. JSON parse error is permanent. Postgres connection timeout is transient. A 4xx from a downstream API is usually permanent (bad request) but a 429 is transient (rate limit). Get this taxonomy right.

**The DLQ topic itself**

```bash
# DLQ topic config
kafka-topics.sh --create --topic orders.dlq \
  --partitions 6 \
  --config retention.ms=2592000000 \   # 30 days
  --config compression.type=lz4 \
  --config min.insync.replicas=2
```

Retain longer than your main topic - DLQ messages need time for humans to investigate.

**Replay tooling**

The DLQ is useless without a way to replay. A simple replay script:

```python
def replay(dlq_topic: str, target_topic: str, since_ms: int) -> None:
    consumer = Consumer({...})
    producer = Producer({...})
    consumer.subscribe([dlq_topic])
    while True:
        msg = consumer.poll(1.0)
        if msg is None:
            break
        if msg.timestamp()[1] < since_ms:
            continue
        # original_value is the body; key preserved
        producer.produce(target_topic, value=msg.value(), key=msg.key())
    producer.flush(60.0)
```

Run after fixing the underlying bug. Idempotent consumers handle re-delivery without harm.

**Observability requirements**

- Metric: `dlq_publishes_total{reason, topic}` - alert if rate exceeds threshold
- Metric: `consumer_lag{topic, partition}` - alert if it stops decreasing
- Log every DLQ publish with the original offset so you can correlate
- Dashboard showing DLQ topic depth - if it's growing, something is systematically broken

If you'd had this in place last week, the first poison pill would have published to the DLQ, advanced the offset, fired a metric, and your on-call would have been paged in minutes instead of hours.

[EN] Category: CODING
Question: This is great. Quick tangent though - we're also evaluating moving off Kafka because the operational burden is heavy. Pulsar and NATS keep coming up. For the kind of consumer patterns we just talked about (DLQ, exactly-once-ish, durable streams), how do they actually compare? Not the marketing version.
Response: <think>
The user wants the honest comparison, not the vendor-pitch version. Let me think about what each system actually delivers for their use case.

**Kafka:** They know it. Strong durability, partitioned log, consumer group protocol with rebalancing. Operational complexity around brokers, ZooKeeper/KRaft, partitions sizing, rebalance behavior. EOS works for Kafka-to-Kafka. Mature ecosystem (Connect, Streams, Schema Registry).

**Pulsar:** Multi-tier architecture - separates compute (brokers) from storage (BookKeeper). Brokers are stateless, easier to scale. Native multi-tenancy, geo-replication built-in. Has 'subscriptions' which can be Exclusive, Shared, Key_Shared, Failover - more flexible than Kafka's consumer groups for some patterns. Built-in DLQ support (`deadLetterPolicy`). Tiered storage to S3 is mature. Tradeoffs: more moving parts (BookKeeper, ZooKeeper, brokers, sometimes Pulsar Functions runtime), smaller community, fewer engineers know it.

**NATS / NATS JetStream:** Originally an in-memory message bus, JetStream added persistence. Lightweight, single-binary, easy to operate. Subjects with wildcards (more flexible routing than Kafka topics). JetStream has streams + consumers (analogous to topics + consumer groups). Pull or push consumers. ACK explicit, with retry on negative ack and configurable max delivery. Built-in support for redelivery counting and 'park' behavior. But: JetStream's durability model is different (replicas, not log compaction primarily), partition model is simpler/coarser, ecosystem is much smaller. EOS is not a thing the way it is in Kafka. Performance is excellent for low-latency request-reply but at very high event-per-sec volumes Kafka still wins.

For their use case (DLQ, durable streams, exactly-once-ish):
- Kafka: full feature support, mature DLQ patterns (manual or via Spring Kafka), full EOS.
- Pulsar: built-in DLQ (`deadLetterPolicy.maxRedeliverCount` + `deadLetterTopic`), 'effective once' (their term), simpler operational model after initial setup, BUT BookKeeper is its own beast.
- NATS JetStream: explicit ack with retry semantics, MaxDeliver setting, can route failed messages to a DLQ stream, but no exactly-once across producer-consumer-database boundaries.

The honest take:
- If they want LESS operational burden, NATS JetStream is the easiest. Single binary, simple config. But they sacrifice some Kafka-specific features (Streams API, Connect ecosystem, mature schema registry).
- If they want a more managed-feeling experience but with serious durability, Pulsar is good but the multi-component architecture is its own complexity. Net operational burden is similar to Kafka in my experience, just shaped differently.
- Kafka's burden often comes from misuse (too many partitions, no monitoring, naive rebalance config). Sometimes the fix is operating Kafka better, not switching.

I should also mention: managed services. Confluent Cloud, MSK, StreamNative for Pulsar, Synadia for NATS. Often the answer is 'switch to managed' before 'switch brokers'.

Let me structure this concretely with a comparison table and an honest recommendation.
</think>

The honest comparison, with operational burden front and center.

**TL;DR**

| | Kafka | Pulsar | NATS JetStream |
|---|---|---|---|
| Durability model | Partitioned log, replicated | Tiered: brokers + BookKeeper | Replicated stream |
| Built-in DLQ | No (you build it) | Yes (`deadLetterPolicy`) | Yes (via stream + max delivery) |
| Exactly-once | Kafka-to-Kafka EOS | 'Effective once' (similar caveats) | At-least-once + ack |
| Operational complexity | High; well-known | High; fewer experts | Lower; smaller scope |
| Ecosystem | Largest (Connect, Streams, schema registry, etc.) | Mid-size | Smaller |
| Community knowledge | Vast | Modest | Modest |
| Best fit for your use case | Mature, but you're feeling the burden | Similar to Kafka, redistributed | Easiest to run |

**Pulsar - the not-so-marketing version**

Pulsar's architecture separates *brokers* (stateless compute) from *BookKeeper* (durable storage). This is genuinely good: you can restart brokers freely, scale them independently, and storage is offloaded to a system designed for it. But:

- BookKeeper is its own distributed system with its own failure modes, monitoring needs, and ZooKeeper dependency. The total operational surface is at least as large as Kafka.
- Pulsar Subscriptions are richer than Kafka consumer groups. `Key_Shared` subscriptions in particular are nice when you want guaranteed key-ordered processing across multiple consumers without the rigidity of partition assignment. For your order-processor pattern, this is a real win.
- Built-in DLQ via `deadLetterPolicy`:
  ```java
  Consumer<byte[]> consumer = client.newConsumer()
      .topic("orders")
      .subscriptionName("order-processor")
      .deadLetterPolicy(DeadLetterPolicy.builder()
          .maxRedeliverCount(5)
          .deadLetterTopic("orders-DLQ")
          .build())
      .negativeAckRedeliveryDelay(30, TimeUnit.SECONDS)
      .subscribe();
  ```
  After 5 negative acks, the message goes to `orders-DLQ` automatically. This eliminates a lot of the boilerplate I showed earlier.
- 'Effective once' is Pulsar's EOS analog. Same caveats as Kafka EOS - it's a Pulsar-internal guarantee, not magic across external systems.
- Tiered storage to S3 is mature. If your retention requirements are long, this is a meaningful cost win vs Kafka.

When Pulsar is worth it: multi-tenant clusters serving many teams, geo-replication needs, long-retention workloads. When it isn't: small/medium org with a few use cases - the operational cost is similar to Kafka with fewer engineers who know how to debug it.

**NATS JetStream - the not-so-marketing version**

NATS itself is a brilliantly simple pub/sub. JetStream adds persistence and is genuinely good for many use cases:

- Single binary, simple config. Operational burden is dramatically lower than Kafka or Pulsar at small-to-medium scale.
- Streams + Consumers model. A 'consumer' has its own ack tracking and `MaxDeliver` setting:
  ```go
  cons, _ := js.PullSubscribe("orders.>", "order-processor",
      nats.MaxDeliver(5),
      nats.AckWait(30*time.Second),
      nats.MaxAckPending(1000),
  )
  ```
  After 5 deliveries, the message is dropped (or you can route it to a DLQ stream via a separate subject).
- Subjects with wildcards (`orders.us.*`, `orders.>`) are far more flexible than Kafka topic names.
- Latency is excellent for low-latency request-reply. JetStream's durability layer is fine for moderate event-streaming volumes (low millions of msgs/sec/cluster); above that, Kafka still wins.
- No EOS in the Kafka sense. Strict at-least-once with explicit ack. For your use case (where you should be doing application-level idempotency anyway, per our earlier discussion), this isn't actually a downgrade.

Where NATS hurts: if you need Kafka-Connect-style ecosystem (CDC connectors, S3 sinks, etc.), it's much sparser. Schema management is mostly DIY. Stream processing isn't really there - no equivalent to Kafka Streams or Flink-on-Pulsar.

**The thing I'd actually do first**

Before switching brokers, ask: is your operational burden from *Kafka* or from *self-hosting Kafka*?

Most teams that report 'Kafka is too operationally heavy' are running their own clusters. Confluent Cloud / MSK Serverless / Aiven Kafka often fix 80% of the pain without a migration. You keep the ecosystem, the Streams API, the body of internet knowledge, and you offload the broker ops.

If the burden is genuinely about Kafka the *system* (rebalances, partition planning, schema registry sprawl), then NATS JetStream is the lowest-friction alternative for moderate scale. Pulsar is a sideways move - different shape of complexity, similar total weight.

**Concrete recommendation for your team**

1. Fix the consumer (the DLQ pattern from earlier) regardless of broker. That solves the actual incident.
2. Quantify your burden: how many on-call pages per month are Kafka-specific? How many partitions do you actually need? What's your peak msgs/sec?
3. If burden is mostly self-hosting: try managed Kafka first.
4. If you genuinely need to switch and your scale is moderate (< low millions msgs/sec): NATS JetStream gives you most of what you need with dramatically less ops.
5. If you need Kafka's ecosystem (Connect, Streams, mature SR) but want a different operational model: look at Pulsar with eyes open about BookKeeper.

The mistake I'd push back on hardest: switching to Pulsar 'because it's modern.' I've seen teams spend a year migrating, only to discover BookKeeper is just as much work as Kafka brokers - they traded familiar pain for unfamiliar pain.
